 B.3 Concretization Lean: gamma [Tier-2___-definitions/Symbolic___Specifications___/Symbolic.Specifications.Refinement.Concretization/#DY___Libsignal___Composed___Refinement___gamma]

 γ\gammaγ (§5.1.4 [#sec-refinement]) is defined by recursion on terms: each function symbol fff comes with a function on byte strings, and γ(f(t1,…,tk))\gamma(f(t_1,\ldots,t_k))γ(f(t1​,…,tk​)) applies it to γ(t1),…,γ(tk)\gamma(t_1),\ldots,\gamma(t_k)γ(t1​),…,γ(tk​).

 Literals stand for their own bytes, concat [https://github.com/BobDyLean/dylean/blob/74b1b2db1f188ba97d84d87637a6d2709a4e7508/DY/EquationalTheory/Concat.lean#L11] appends, trunc8 [Tier-2___-definitions/Symbolic___Specifications___/Symbolic.Specifications.Model.EquationalTheory.Trunc/#DY___Trunc___CanTrunc8___trunc8] keeps the first 8 bytes, and the wire-message symbols give libsignal’s protobuf encoding. The cryptographic symbols use an interpretation I\mathcal{I}I, which gives each symbol’s result on its argument bytes. dhPk [https://github.com/BobDyLean/dylean/blob/74b1b2db1f188ba97d84d87637a6d2709a4e7508/DY/EquationalTheory/DiffieHellman.lean#L12] and dh [https://github.com/BobDyLean/dylean/blob/74b1b2db1f188ba97d84d87637a6d2709a4e7508/DY/EquationalTheory/DiffieHellman.lean#L13] are X25519, symEnc [Tier-2___-definitions/Symbolic___Specifications___/Symbolic.Specifications.Model.EquationalTheory.SymEnc/#DY___SymEnc___CanSymEnc___symEnc] is AES-256-CBC, Encap is Kyber1024 encapsulation, and kdfExtract [https://github.com/BobDyLean/dylean/blob/74b1b2db1f188ba97d84d87637a6d2709a4e7508/DY/EquationalTheory/KdfExtract.lean#L11] is HMAC-SHA256, built in Lean from the SHA-256 compression function. Among the laws assumed of I\mathcal{I}I is Diffie-Hellman commutativity: dh [https://github.com/BobDyLean/dylean/blob/74b1b2db1f188ba97d84d87637a6d2709a4e7508/DY/EquationalTheory/DiffieHellman.lean#L13] sorts its two secrets, so both parties compute the same shared-secret term, and commutativity gives them the same bytes. The remaining laws state that X25519 public keys are canonical and never low-order, that agreement reads only the u-coordinate, ignores torsion points and is zero exactly on low-order points, that the binary’s torsion check rejects a public key shifted by a torsion point (without this law the theorem is false, since the MAC does not cover the base key), that AES-CBC decryption inverts encryption and has no junk (a ciphertext that decrypts is the encryption of its plaintext), and that X25519 outputs and libcrux HMAC tags are 32 bytes long. These laws are trusted; we prove only that they are jointly satisfiable.

 Lean definition: symEnc

 classDY.SymEnc.CanSymEnc Symbolic.Specifications.Model.EquationalTheory.SymEnc (L26–29) → [Tier-2___-definitions/Symbolic___Specifications___/Symbolic.Specifications.Model.EquationalTheory.SymEnc/#DY___SymEnc___CanSymEnc]

 public
class CanSymEnc (Bytes: Type u) where
 symEnc: (key: Bytes) → (iv: Bytes) → (msg: Bytes) → Bytes
 symDec: (key: Bytes) → (iv: Bytes) → (ct: Bytes) → Err Bytes

 Two more parameters complete γ\gammaγ. Some symbols are opaque: the SPQR operations, Kyber1024 public keys, decapsulation of a ciphertext that is not an honest encapsulation, and XEdDSA. Their bytes come from fixed byte functions SSS, which must agree with the setup: they give the setup’s signatures and Kyber public key their provisioned bytes, and decapsulation under Bob’s Kyber prekey agrees with I\mathcal{I}I. Nothing else is assumed about them. A per-trace ghost ggg gives the bytes of each fresh value: those of the piece of a run’s coins, or of the plaintext of a send run, that it is matched with, or, for the setup’s keys, the setup’s bytes. It also gives the bytes of each wire message’s addresses field, which has no model term. The ghost is the only part of γ\gammaγ that depends on the execution.

 B.4 Explanations Lean: MatchesEvents [Tier-2___-definitions/Symbolic___Specifications___/Symbolic.Specifications.Refinement.TraceExplanation/#DY___Libsignal___Composed___Refinement___MatchesEvents] Lean: Explains [Tier-2___-definitions/Symbolic___Specifications___/Symbolic.Specifications.Refinement.TraceExplanation/#DY___Libsignal___Composed___Refinement___Explains]

 Each transition along a model trace (§5.1.1 [#sec-model]) corresponds to at most one event from Table 4 [#tab-events], carrying symbolic terms rather than bytes: for example, encrypting a message corresponds to a send run whose input is a fresh plaintext term, whose coins are fresh coins, and whose output is a wire-message term; compromising a secret corresponds to a leak; and a rejected run leaves the model state unchanged. A model trace matches a hardware trace τ\tauτ if these events line up with τ\tauτ’s events one to one, in order, and each byte field of a hardware event equals γ\gammaγ applied to the corresponding model term(s) under libsignal’s wire and key encodings.

 A model trace explains τ\tauτ if it is reachable in the model, matches τ\tauτ, and agrees with the execution on details that the hardware events do not record. Because τ\tauτ records only what goes into and out of each run, it omits how stored session records overwrite prior state from run to run, and how incoming bytes were decoded and routed internally. Pinning explanations to these unlogged details prevents the model trace from reusing replaced session state or logging handshakes the binary did not perform when we evaluate the security queries on it, and—in the collision clause below—rules out degenerate explanations of a trace prefix.

 B.5 The Collision Clause

 The collision clause of the refinement definition (§5.1.4 [#sec-refinement]) excuses only runs that no explanation can account for without a collision between two different terms, at least one built by a symbol assumed injective. No opaque symbol is assumed injective. Two further clauses concern the truncated MAC: a received message is also excused if its 8-byte MAC equals one that the receiver computes from a ratchet seed the attacker does not know (a guessed MAC), or one computed under a different seed. Three features constrain the collision case. First, SSS is chosen before the hardware trace, and opaque bytes are never assumed injective, so SSS cannot be tuned to create collisions. Second, for a received message the collision must occur under every explanation of the prefix that has not already read a colliding message (except in the Kyber case below), so the proof cannot pick an explanation that happens to collide. Third, a received message counts as a collision only if every term the attacker knows that the model would accept for it itself involves a collision: once the attacker has leaked or inverted a key, a message it forges with that key must be explained, not excused.

 One further clause concerns Kyber: a decapsulation that disagrees with encapsulation is excused, since no Kyber correctness law is assumed on I\mathcal{I}I; hardware traces in this case get no guarantee. The clause reads only the interpretation I\mathcal{I}I, the setup and the received ciphertext, never SSS, the ghost or the run, so it holds only if decapsulation under Bob’s provisioned key disagrees with encapsulation on that ciphertext: the hooked Kyber implementation is incorrect, the provisioned key is malformed (key generation is not verified, and well-formedness checks only the public key embedded in the secret key), or the ciphertext is one of the decryption failures that Kyber1024 has with negligible probability. (Kyber’s implicit rejection of a malformed ciphertext is modeled directly in the symbolic KEM by a secret-keyed rejection symbol, so it is explained rather than excused.)

 B.6 The Security Statement

 For initiator secrecy, for instance, the query in Q(tr,K)Q(\mathit{tr}, K)Q(tr,K) says that if Alice completed a handshake in tr\mathit{tr}tr and one of its keys is in KKK, then one of that row’s excuses of Table 5 [#tab-queries] holds on tr\mathit{tr}tr. The ten properties differ only in QQQ, where each is one conjunct: stated alone, each has exactly the shape of Theorem 1 [#thm-sym] with its own query in place of QQQ, and Theorem 1 [#thm-sym] implies all ten of these statements, with one common SSS, because QQQ is their conjunction. Table 5 [#tab-queries] states every query using the event shorthands defined next (for the events of Table 3 [#tab-composed-transitions]).

 A handshake query fixes the session’s parties aaa (Alice) and bbb (Bob), its keys ikA\mathit{ikA}ikA, ikB\mathit{ikB}ikB, spk\mathit{spk}spk, pqpk\mathit{pqpk}pqpk, opk\mathit{opk}opk and outputs keys={rk,ck,pqr}\mathit{keys}=\{\mathit{rk},\mathit{ck},\mathit{pqr}\}keys={rk,ck,pqr}, and the index iii of Alice’s AliceDone [Tier-2___-definitions/Symbolic___Specifications___/Symbolic.Specifications.Model.PQXDH/#DY___Libsignal___PQXDH___PQXDHEvent___AliceDone] or Bob’s BobDone [Tier-2___-definitions/Symbolic___Specifications___/Symbolic.Specifications.Model.PQXDH/#DY___Libsignal___PQXDH___PQXDHEvent___BobDone] event; Done\mathsf{Done}Done is either. IKA\mathsf{IK}_AIKA​, IKB\mathsf{IK}_BIKB​, SPK\mathsf{SPK}SPK, PQ\mathsf{PQ}PQ, OPK\mathsf{OPK}OPK are the compromise events of the session’s identity keys and prekeys, and DH\mathsf{DH}DH, KEM\mathsf{KEM}KEM the breaks. An event EEE alone means EEE is logged anywhere on tr\mathit{tr}tr; E<iE^{<i}E<i and E≤iE^{\le i}E≤i, before and at or before iii. Exp(k)\mathsf{Exp}(k)Exp(k) (SessionStateExposed [Tier-2___-definitions/Symbolic___Specifications___/Symbolic.Specifications.Security.Model/#DY___Libsignal___PQXDH___SessionStateExposed]) holds if block kkk itself was exposed from a session started with keys\mathit{keys}keys. For the ratchet, mmm is a used message seed with SPQR salt sss; ck⇝m\mathit{ck}\leadsto mck⇝m and rk⇝ck\mathit{rk}\leadsto\mathit{ck}rk⇝ck mean derivation by chain and root ratchet steps, and rk⪯rk′\mathit{rk}\preceq\mathit{rk}'rk⪯rk′ that rk′\mathit{rk}'rk′ descends from rk\mathit{rk}rk. C(x)C(x)C(x) (CompromisedKey [Tier-2___-definitions/Symbolic___Specifications___/Symbolic.Specifications.Security.Model/#DY___Libsignal___TripleRatchet___Cheval___CompromisedKey]) holds if xxx was exposed, or is a handshake’s initial rk\mathit{rk}rk or ck\mathit{ck}ck and every handshake deriving it is excused by initiator or responder secrecy, or is an honest DH ratchet secret and DH\mathsf{DH}DH holds.

 B.7 Scope of the Binary Theorem

 The hardware traces fix a narrower scenario than the model; on other traces the theorem says nothing. The main restrictions are as follows (the Lean statement adds technical conditions on file paths). Alice and Bob each run on one device, and each starts with the other’s identity key pinned. Bob has one signed prekey, one reusable last-resort Kyber prekey and at most one one-time prekey; one-time Kyber prekeys are not covered. Only Alice runs init, and only when she has no session; only Bob receives prekey messages. The bundle Alice receives carries Bob’s genuine identity key, signed prekey and Kyber prekey with their signatures (the unsigned one-time prekey is unconstrained), and received SPQR fields come from earlier honest messages. A leak reveals only Alice’s identity secret, Bob’s identity, signed-prekey and Kyber secrets, his one-time prekey while unused, and the keys of a party’s current session. Inputs are below 2322^{32}232 bytes (a prekey message’s inner message below 232−20482^{32}-2048232−2048), files below 2312^{31}231 bytes, coins files exactly 192, 64 and 32 bytes for init, send and recv, and there are fewer than 232−12^{32}-1232−1 sends. After Bob accepts a prekey message that does not match his current session, he makes no call until the environment deletes his session file. The theorem also assumes fresh coins (§5.1.5 [#sec-sym-main]). Within this scenario the attacker schedules every run, controls the network, may delete a party’s session file, and may leak secrets and break DH and the KEM, as in the model.

 B.8 Refinement Preservation

 The preservation theorem is proved once, independently of the binary: for every predicate relating the hooks to I\mathcal{I}I and every run relation, the ten model theorems and the refinement definition of §5.1.4 [#sec-refinement] imply the statement of Theorem 1 [#thm-sym]. The machine code enters only through the refinement theorem, so a new build of libsignal-cli [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/third_party/libsignal/libsignal-session/cli/src/lib.rs#L168-L179] needs a new refinement proof but no new security proof.

 The proof has two steps. First, every explaining model trace is reachable, so each model theorem applies to it. Second, every term that the hardware attacker derives under an explanation is known to the model attacker on the explaining trace. Only this second step takes real work: each leaked or inverted secret matches a model step that reveals it, and two kinds of terms need model invariants showing that they already reached the attacker: the ephemeral key and Kyber ciphertext that Alice’s binary resends in every prekey message while her prekey is pending, and Bob’s one-time prekey.

 The queries then transfer because of their shape. Each one either ignores the attacker’s knowledge (authentication, peer authentication, KCI) or uses it only as a hypothesis (“if the attacker knows this key, then an excuse holds”), and every excuse is an event of the model trace, never a fact about knowledge. So an attacker that knows less cannot break a query. A query whose excuse read “the attacker knows kkk” would not transfer this way.

 In the collision case, preservation still gives the ten queries on every explanation of the prefix before the colliding run, so a collision voids the guarantees only from that run on.

 C Computational Proofs

 This appendix gives the precise game (§C.1 [#app-game]), the cost semantics of reductions (§C.2 [#app-reductions]), the four lemmas (§C.3 [#app-lemmas]), the full main theorem and its proof (§C.4 [#app-main]), and the assumptions and restrictions in detail (§C.5 [#app-assm], §C.6 [#app-limitations]). The remaining subsections list the premises on the primitives and the terms of the single-session bound.

 C.1 The Composed Game

 The challenger holds every party’s state (in the game on the executable, the party’s directory) and plays the server that distributes identity keys and prekeys. It also supplies the coins of every run, except that the adversary chooses the coins of sends.

 The game is written once, as a generic game G(Σ)G(\Sigma)G(Σ) parameterized by a protocol interface Σ\SigmaΣ that supplies the handshake and ratchet steps. The oracles and the winning condition consult Σ\SigmaΣ only through these steps, for example to decide whether an injected message is accepted. We write G(ΣmodelH)G(\Sigma^H_{\mathrm{model}})G(ΣmodelH​) for the game whose interface is the byte-level model with handshake HHH, and G(Σbin)G(\Sigma_{\mathrm{bin}})G(Σbin​) for the game whose interface is runs of libsignal-cli [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/third_party/libsignal/libsignal-session/cli/src/lib.rs#L168-L179]. The advantage of AAA is 2 ∣Pr⁡[A wins]−12∣2\,|\Pr[A\text{ wins}]-\tfrac12|2∣Pr[A wins]−21​∣, written Advcomp(A)\mathsf{Adv}^{\mathrm{comp}}(A)Advcomp(A) in G(ΣmodelH)G(\Sigma^H_{\mathrm{model}})G(ΣmodelH​) and Advbin(A)\mathsf{Adv}^{\mathrm{bin}}(A)Advbin(A) in G(Σbin)G(\Sigma_{\mathrm{bin}})G(Σbin​). In the main text, Advcomp\mathsf{Adv}^{\mathrm{comp}}Advcomp refers to H=HlibH=H_{\mathrm{lib}}H=Hlib​.

 Handshake oracles. These are the oracles of the PQXDH key-indistinguishability game of [52 [#bib-fg24]]: registration of honest parties and of adversarial keys (whose secret keys the adversary must supply), generation of signed and one-time prekeys, initiation and acceptance, corruption of identity keys, reveal of session keys and of signed-prekey secrets, and, if the handshake models the session-key HKDF as a random oracle, queries to that oracle. The handshake itself is a parameter HHH.

 Promotion. The test query of the key-indistinguishability game is withheld from the adversary. Instead, the adversary may promote an initiator session sidsidsid by calling startDR(sid)\mathsf{startDR}(sid)startDR(sid). The oracle checks that sidsidsid was initiated through the game and not yet promoted. It checks that sidsidsid used, byte for byte, the identity key, signed prekey, Kyber prekey and (if any) one-time prekey that a registered party published, with matching prekey identifiers, and that no earlier instance used the same ephemeral key. If so, it initializes the next ratchet instance, including SPQR, for both parties with the real session key. Finally, it marks the responder’s signed prekey used in the handshake as revealed in the handshake game; the secret goes to the ratchet, not to the adversary.

 Ratchet oracles. Each ratchet instance has the oracles of the ACD19 secure-messaging game [7 [#bib-alwen2019doubleratchet]]: transmit, challenge (m0,m1)(m_0,m_1)(m0​,m1​), deliver, inject (a forgery attempt) and corruption of either party’s state. The adversary wins if it guesses the challenge bit bbb or gets a non-trivial forgery accepted, subject to ACD19’s cleanness conditions and the further restrictions of §C.6 [#app-limitations]. If at the end the handshake part of the execution violates the freshness condition of [52 [#bib-fg24]], the game outputs a random bit.

 SPQR. The ratchet in each session is libsignal’s Triple Ratchet: the Double Ratchet together with SPQR. SPQR carries ML-KEM-768 exchanges in chunks inside the message headers and, as they complete, outputs fresh 32-byte keys. Each message key is derived from the Double Ratchet’s chain key with the current SPQR key as the HKDF salt. SPQR lives in the protocol interface, not in the oracles, but the oracles reach it in four ways:

 startDR\mathsf{startDR}startDR derives SPQR’s initial secret from the handshake key, along with the Double Ratchet’s;

 adversarially chosen send coins are also SPQR’s coins, including those for ML-KEM key generation and encapsulation;

 corrupting a party leaks its SPQR state with the rest of its ratchet state;

 injected messages may carry arbitrary SPQR headers.

 Handshakes. Both HlibH_{\mathrm{lib}}Hlib​ and HroH_{\mathrm{ro}}Hro​ are one-key handshakes with Kyber1024 (round 3) as the KEM. Prior analyses [52 [#bib-fg24], 28 [#bib-bhargavan2024pqxdh]] give each party two independent identity keys, one for Diffie-Hellman and one for signing. Standalone Lean proofs for that two-key model, and for its first-message authentication game, are preserved outside the trusted base.

 Budgets. As in [52 [#bib-fg24]], the handshake budgets are per party. Each of at most NidN_{\mathrm{id}}Nid​ honest parties publishes a bounded number of signed-prekey bundles, each bundle a bounded number of one-time prekeys and of sessions accepted with and without one, and each party initiates a bounded number of sessions with and without a one-time prekey. We write NspkN_{\mathrm{spk}}Nspk​, NopkN_{\mathrm{opk}}Nopk​, NsendN_{\mathrm{send}}Nsend​ and NaccN_{\mathrm{acc}}Nacc​ for the resulting totals of signed prekeys, one-time prekeys, initiated sessions and accepted sessions, Nsend,opk\leNsendN_{\mathrm{send,opk}}\leN_{\mathrm{send}}Nsend,opk​\leNsend​ for the initiated sessions that use a one-time prekey, and Ndh=Nid+Nspk+Nopk+NsendN_{\mathrm{dh}}=N_{\mathrm{id}}+N_{\mathrm{spk}}+N_{\mathrm{opk}}+N_{\mathrm{send}}Ndh​=Nid​+Nspk​+Nopk​+Nsend​ for the number of X25519 key pairs that the challenger generates. The adversary makes at most qhsq_{\mathrm{hs}}qhs​ handshake queries in total, of which at most qonq_{\mathrm{on}}qon​ are not random-oracle queries (with separate caps on random-oracle queries), and qstartq_{\mathrm{start}}qstart​ promotions. The kkk-th promoted session allows at most qkq_kqk​ ratchet queries, where 2(qk+1)+25002<2322(q_k+1)+25002<2^{32}2(qk​+1)+25002<232, and qepq_{\mathrm{ep}}qep​ epochs (ACD19’s epoch cap).

 C.2 Reductions and Their Costs

 A reduction RRR runs the adversary AAA exactly once, as a black box and without rewinding. It prepares AAA’s input, answers each of AAA’s oracle queries by running a handler, and computes its own output from AAA’s. A handler may compute on ordinary data (numbers, lists, tables), sample uniform coins and query the oracles of the assumption game. It operates on keys and group elements only through declared primitive operations, and it loops only up to a bound written in the program. If the assumption game has a challenge phase, RRR may also pause AAA at one query, send a value to the game, and resume the same run of AAA with the game’s answer.

 Costs are computed from the program text alone. Primitive operations, samples and oracle queries are charged from a fixed table per primitive. The cost of RRR against an adversary that makes qqq game queries is

 cost(R,q)  =  cinit+q⋅max⁡(ccoin, chandle+ccodec)+cfinish,\mathrm{cost}(R,q) \;=\; c_{\mathrm{init}} + q\cdot\max(c_{\mathrm{coin}},\,c_{\mathrm{handle}}+c_{\mathrm{codec}}) + c_{\mathrm{finish}},cost(R,q)=cinit​+q⋅max(ccoin​,chandle​+ccodec​)+cfinish​,

 the syntactic costs of setup, of forwarding a coin or answering one query (including its encoding), and of the output step. For reductions that pause AAA, the handlers before and after the pause, and the pause step itself, enter through maxima. The cost bounds RRR’s work outside AAA, and a similar count, made syntactically or stated over the run, bounds RRR’s queries to the assumption oracles. RRR is cost-bounded if three conditions hold. First, its cost and its query count are bounded by explicit polynomials in the game’s budgets, independent of AAA. Second, no primitive operation or sample produces values larger than its declared cost allows, so that unit costs cannot hide large data; hence, on every run in which AAA’s queries and the oracle answers have bounded size, all values that RRR computes have bounded size too. Third, its program has at most 224+212(⌊log⁡2b⌋+1)2^{24}+2^{12}(\lfloor\log_2 b\rfloor+1)224+212(⌊log2​b⌋+1) nodes, where bbb is its cost budget, so that RRR cannot carry a large table, such as a table of discrete logarithms. A few fixed wrappers in our statements lie outside this language (§C.6 [#app-limitations]). They are part of the trusted base.

 Lemma 1 (PQXDH).  Here δkem\delta_{\mathrm{kem}}δkem​ bounds the probability of a decapsulation failure, averaged over honest key-generation and encapsulation coins.  Lemma 2 [#lem-session] concerns one ratchet session in isolation. Let SM(idA,idB,s)\mathrm{SM}(\mathit{id}_A,\mathit{id}_B,s)SM(idA​,idB​,s) be the ACD19 secure-messaging game with epoch cap qepq_{\mathrm{ep}}qep​ (and ACD19’s delay parameter Δ=3\Delta=3Δ=3) on a single Triple Ratchet session between identity keys idA,idB\mathit{id}_A,\mathit{id}_BidA​,idB​, whose initial keys are uniform and whose responder’s signed-prekey secret is derived from coins sss and leaked.  where ooo is drawn once before the game runs. Lemma 2 [#lem-session] is stated for priors rather than fixed contexts so that most assumptions are charged the advantage of a single reduction.2 [#fn-2]

 The bound Sess(O)\mathsf{Sess}(\mathcal{O})Sess(O), given in full in §C.9 [#app-sess], has three kinds of terms. Some follow ACD19’s analysis of the Double Ratchet core, with CKA, PRF-PRNG and FS-AEAD terms. Some cover SPQR and the leaked signed prekey, with HKDF PRF and PRG terms. The rest are statistical terms that do not depend on O\mathcal{O}O. Each computational term is one of three kinds: the advantage of an adversary that samples o←Oo\leftarrow\mathcal{O}o←O and runs AoA_oAo​ through a member of DDD (in some cases behind a simulator fixed in the statement), the advantage of such an adversary run through a fixed program, or, for the hidden-salt terms, the average over ooo of the advantage of AoA_oAo​ run through a member of DDD. Only the members of DDD are cost-bounded in the lemma. The fixed programs are explicit; apart from a few wrappers (§C.6 [#app-limitations]), they are written in the costed language.

 Lemma 3 [#lem-comp] reduces the security of the composed game to the key indistinguishability of the handshake and to single-session games, one per promoted session, against priors that the reduction constructs. It holds for any handshake HHH that implements the oracles of the key-indistinguishability game of [52 [#bib-fg24]] and satisfies a lazy-sampling condition, defined below. We write KIH(B)\mathsf{KI}_H(B)KIH​(B) for the advantage of BBB in that game run with HHH’s oracles.

 Lemma 3 (Composition).  for each k<qstartk<q_{\mathrm{start}}k<qstart​ and bit fff, running RkpreR^{\mathrm{pre}}_kRkpre​ with AAA up to its pause (which does not depend on fff) and then resuming it with bit fff yields a prior Ok(f)\mathcal{O}_k(f)Ok​(f) in whose support every adversary makes at most qkq_kqk​ queries; and

 if HHH satisfies the lazy-sampling condition for AAA, then

 Advcomp(A)  ≤  2 KIH(RKIA)  +  ∑k<qstart max⁡f∈{0,1}AdvSM(Ok(f)).\mathsf{Adv}^{\mathrm{comp}}(A) \;\le\; 2\,\mathsf{KI}_H(R_{\mathrm{KI}}^A) \;+\; \sum_{k<q_{\mathrm{start}}}\ \max_{f\in\{0,1\}} \mathsf{Adv}^{\mathrm{SM}}\bigl(\mathcal{O}_k(f)\bigr).Advcomp(A)≤2KIH​(RKIA​)+k<qstart​∑​ f∈{0,1}max​AdvSM(Ok​(f)).

 The lazy-sampling condition concerns HHH together with the game’s driver. Fix k<qstartk<q_{\mathrm{start}}k<qstart​ and a bit bkb_kbk​, and consider the hybrid in which ratchet instances i<ki<ki<k use challenge bit 1, instance kkk uses bit bkb_kbk​ and instances i>ki>ki>k use bit 0, run against HHH in the random-key world. The condition requires that this hybrid has the same output distribution whether instance kkk is keyed with the key derived from HHH’s test answer or with a fresh uniform key. A condition on HHH alone (“a fresh key in place of one tested key is invisible”) would fail for every partner-consistent handshake, since the adversary could test both partners. The driver withholds the test query and promotes at most one instance per ephemeral key. We prove the condition for HlibH_{\mathrm{lib}}Hlib​ for every adversary.

 Lemma 3 [#lem-comp] does not use P1–P10. Its per-session terms are raw single-session biases, to which Lemma 2 [#lem-session] is then applied with prior Ok(f)\mathcal{O}_k(f)Ok​(f). We write Sessk=max⁡fSess(Ok(f))\mathsf{Sess}_k=\max_f\mathsf{Sess}(\mathcal{O}_k(f))Sessk​=maxf​Sess(Ok​(f)).

 Lemma 4 [#lem-refine] concerns the machine code. Let enc\mathsf{enc}enc encode a model state as a party’s directory (the files of Figure 4 [#fig-cli-state], with each session stored as libsignal’s serialized record), and let obs\mathsf{obs}obs map a model outcome to an exit code, a standard output and new file contents. A party state σ\sigmaσ is well-formed if its session, if any, is one reachable from a PQXDH handshake followed by ratchet steps, every file of enc(σ)\mathsf{enc}(\sigma)enc(σ) is below 2312^{31}231 bytes, and its prekey identifiers of each kind are distinct. For an operation op\mathsf{op}op (init with no session, send, or recv of a prekey message in protobuf’s canonical encoding, of an ordinary message, or of anything else), step\mathsf{step}step is its model function and run\mathsf{run}run runs the executable from the state in which Linux has loaded it to its exit_group system call.

 Lemma 4 (Machine code refines the model).  theorem libsignal_cli_pq_binary_correct_placed :
 CliPqxdhBinaryCorrectPlaced [Tier-1___-statements/Statement-files-_LPAR_T_RPAR_/LibsignalBinary.Specifications.SignalSession.CliBinaryPq/#CryptoVerification___SignalSession___CliPqxdhBinaryCorrectPlaced] cliElfBytes [Tier-1___-statements/Statement-files-_LPAR_T_RPAR_/LibsignalBinary.Specifications.SignalSession.CliElf/#CryptoVerification___SignalSession___cliElfBytes] := ⋯

 “No terminal state” is an outcome that the model never produces, so clause 1 includes termination. A rejected operation exits with code 1 and writes no file. The exception arises because, when it accepts such a message, libsignal archives the old session in the new record, which our encoding does not represent. The game never reaches the accepted case: the initiator refuses every prekey message, and the responder accepts one over an existing session only if its header is that of the session’s handshake. The lemma assumes nothing about the hooked primitives. That the real routines behave as their Lean specifications is part of the trusted base (§5.3 [#sec-tcb]). The lemma is functional refinement of whole runs, and intermediate machine states are unconstrained. For SPQR, step\mathsf{step}step uses our Lean model of spqr 1.6.0, which is trusted. The lemma fixes every output, including on rejected and adversarial inputs, so any deviation from the model, harmful or not, contradicts it, whether it comes from the Rust source or the compiler.

 Proof outline. The proof is compositional, in a separation logic for AArch64. Each compiled function, including inlined Rust library code (Vec, protobuf, SessionRecord serialization, SPQR), the CLI’s argument handling and file-backed stores, and musl’s start-up and exit code and its mallocng allocator, gets a contract relating its registers and memory footprint to the corresponding model function. Contracts are proved by symbolic execution with a weakest-precondition calculus (callee contracts, hook specifications, loop invariants, the frame rule, and bit-vector side conditions proved in Lean’s kernel). Since the semantics is deterministic, the contract of the whole run determines its unique terminal state. □\square□

 C.4 The Main Theorem in Full

 Let Σbin\Sigma_{\mathrm{bin}}Σbin​ be the interface in which every ratchet step is a run of libsignal-cli [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/third_party/libsignal/libsignal-session/cli/src/lib.rs#L168-L179] in the party’s directory, with messages passed on standard input and output. On startDR\mathsf{startDR}startDR, the initiator runs init, and the responder’s first recv processes her prekey message. The challenger writes no session file, so every session record a party holds was written by its own run of the executable. The handshake is libsignal’s, HlibH_{\mathrm{lib}}Hlib​, and εmlkem(A)\varepsilon_{\mathrm{mlkem}}(A)εmlkem​(A) is as in §5.2.3 [#sec-comp-thms]. The following is the full statement of Theorem 2 [#thm-main].

 Theorem 2 [#thm-main] (Binary security, full statement). For every placement of the library and of musl’s allocator that matches the bytes of the executable, all handshake budgets and every fixed message length ℓ<106\ell<10^6ℓ<106 (the plaintext length of every ratchet message), there are an adapter, a reduction that turns an adversary of G(Σbin)G(\Sigma_{\mathrm{bin}})G(Σbin​) into one of G(ΣmodelHlib)G(\Sigma^{H_{\mathrm{lib}}}_{\mathrm{model}})G(ΣmodelHlib​​), and reductions, all cost-bounded for all query budgets, such that for all hooked primitives whose derived handshake primitives satisfy the well-formedness conditions of §C.7 [#app-wf] and decapsulate correctly with probability at least 1−δkem1-\delta_{\mathrm{kem}}1−δkem​, and that satisfy P1–P10 (§C.8 [#app-prem]), for all δ\deltaδ as in Lemma 2 [#lem-session], all query budgets with 2(qk+1)+25002<2322(q_k+1)+25002<2^{32}2(qk​+1)+25002<232 for every session, and every adversary AAA of G(Σbin)G(\Sigma_{\mathrm{bin}})G(Σbin​) respecting the budgets:

 Security.

 Advbin(A)  ≤  2 εKI+2 NsendNspk δkem+∑k<qstartSessk+2 εmlkem(A),\mathsf{Adv}^{\mathrm{bin}}(A) \;\le\; 2\,\varepsilon_{\mathsf{KI}} + 2\,N_{\mathrm{send}}N_{\mathrm{spk}}\,\delta_{\mathrm{kem}} + \textstyle\sum_{k<q_{\mathrm{start}}} \mathsf{Sess}_k + 2\,\varepsilon_{\mathrm{mlkem}}(A),Advbin(A)≤2εKI​+2Nsend​Nspk​δkem​+∑k<qstart​​Sessk​+2εmlkem​(A),

 where εKI=KIHlib(RKIA′)\varepsilon_{\mathsf{KI}} = \mathsf{KI}_{H_{\mathrm{lib}}}(R_{\mathrm{KI}}^{A'})εKI​=KIHlib​​(RKIA′​) and Sessk\mathsf{Sess}_kSessk​ are as in Lemma 3 [#lem-comp] and Lemma 2 [#lem-session] for the adversary A′A'A′ that runs AAA through the adapter.

 If moreover KIHlib(RKIA′)≤KIHro(RKIA′)+εro\mathsf{KI}_{H_{\mathrm{lib}}}(R_{\mathrm{KI}}^{A'}) \le \mathsf{KI}_{H_{\mathrm{ro}}}(R_{\mathrm{KI}}^{A'}) + \varepsilon_{\mathrm{ro}}KIHlib​​(RKIA′​)≤KIHro​​(RKIA′​)+εro​ (the random-oracle idealization, §C.8 [#p-random-oracle-idealization-not-a-premise]), then the bound of clause 1 holds with 2 εKI2\,\varepsilon_{\mathsf{KI}}2εKI​ replaced by 2(βKIδ+εro)2(\beta_{\mathsf{KI}}^{\delta} + \varepsilon_{\mathrm{ro}})2(βKIδ​+εro​), where βKIδ\beta_{\mathsf{KI}}^{\delta}βKIδ​ is the bound of Lemma 1 [#lem-pqxdh] for B=RKIA′B=R_{\mathrm{KI}}^{A'}B=RKIA′​.

 The bound is that of Lemma 3 [#lem-comp], with each AdvSM\mathsf{Adv}^{\mathrm{SM}}AdvSM term replaced by Sess\mathsf{Sess}Sess, plus the decapsulation term, and with the per-session ML-KEM-768 terms of Lemma 2 [#lem-session] replaced by the single term 2 εmlkem(A)2\,\varepsilon_{\mathrm{mlkem}}(A)2εmlkem​(A). In clause 2, the Kyber1024 decapsulation failure probability enters twice, inside βKIδ\beta_{\mathsf{KI}}^{\delta}βKIδ​ and as the separate term. These are different bad events, one in the key-indistinguishability game and one in the coupling below. Every reachable session record encodes to at most 1,209,3311{,}209{,}3311,209,331 bytes, whatever qkq_kqk​ is, and every honest input is below 2322^{32}232 bytes, so the size premises of Lemma 4 [#lem-refine] hold on every run. Injected inputs must be below 2322^{32}232 bytes, and the inner message of an injected prekey message below 232−20482^{32}-2048232−2048 bytes, leaving room for the wrapper. The game refuses other inputs without running the executable. The reductions of Lemma 1 [#lem-pqxdh] and Lemma 2 [#lem-session] are fixed before the hooked primitives. The adapter depends only on the public-key encoding, and the composition reductions of Lemma 3 [#lem-comp] depend on all primitives, because they run the code of the other sessions.

 Proof. The Lean proof combines Lemma 1 [#lem-pqxdh], Lemma 4 [#lem-refine] and the no-out-of-memory theorem (§4.1 [#sec-binary-guarantees]), used through their statements, with a composition argument that follows Lemma 3 [#lem-comp] and Lemma 2 [#lem-session] but is carried out directly, in a game that stops at the first ML-KEM-768 decryption failure. This is needed because the ML-KEM-768 term of Lemma 2 [#lem-session] is evaluated on adversaries built by the composition reductions, which can trigger a failure with probability 1; the halted game charges the failure once, on AAA itself.

 Correctness of every run. By Lemma 4 [#lem-refine], every run in the game (init, the first prekey recv, and every later send and recv) has the model’s outcome unless the machine runs out of memory, which the no-out-of-memory theorem rules out. This includes the compiled spqr state machine, which computes what our Lean model of SPQR predicts. Only its ML-KEM-768 and HMAC routines are hooked (§4 [#sec-interface]).

 Coupling. The binary game stores directories, and the model game stores model states. The coupling invariant is that every directory is enc(σ)\mathsf{enc}(\sigma)enc(σ) for the corresponding reachable σ\sigmaσ, and (i) preserves it at every oracle call. It breaks only if a Kyber1024 decapsulation in an honest handshake fails, which costs at most 2 NsendNspk δkem2\,N_{\mathrm{send}}N_{\mathrm{spk}}\,\delta_{\mathrm{kem}}2Nsend​Nspk​δkem​. Otherwise AAA in G(Σbin)G(\Sigma_{\mathrm{bin}})G(Σbin​) and A′A'A′ in G(ΣmodelHlib)G(\Sigma^{H_{\mathrm{lib}}}_{\mathrm{model}})G(ΣmodelHlib​​) win with the same probability, and A′A'A′ respects the same budgets.

 Security of the model. Bound the advantage of A′A'A′ by the hybrid argument of Lemma 3 [#lem-comp] with H=HlibH=H_{\mathrm{lib}}H=Hlib​, whose lazy-sampling condition holds for every adversary, and each per-session term as in Lemma 2 [#lem-session] with q=qkq=q_kq=qk​, in the game halted at the first ML-KEM-768 failure; reaching that failure costs at most 2 εmlkem(A)2\,\varepsilon_{\mathrm{mlkem}}(A)2εmlkem​(A). Clause 2 follows by applying Lemma 1 [#lem-pqxdh] to RKIA′R_{\mathrm{KI}}^{A'}RKIA′​. □\square□

 C.5 Assumptions in Detail

 Joint security. Because libsignal uses a single Curve25519 scalar per party for both X25519 Diffie-Hellman and XEdDSA bundle signatures [79 [#bib-perrin2016xeddsa]], Lemma 1 [#lem-pqxdh] pairs its signature and Diffie-Hellman games following standard joint-security definitions [46 [#bib-degabriele2012joint], 95 [#bib-thormarker2021ed25519]]. XEdDSA EUF-CMA gives the adversary the X25519 decisional DH oracle (GapSigEufCma [Tier-1___-statements/Statement-files-_LPAR_T_RPAR_/Computational.Specifications.Assumptions.Assumptions/#SignalPqxdh___GapSigEufCma]), and single-pair X25519 Gap-DH gives the adversary an XEdDSA signing oracle on the first challenge key (GapDhWithSign [Tier-1___-statements/Statement-files-_LPAR_T_RPAR_/Computational.Specifications.Assumptions.Assumptions/#SignalPqxdh___GapDhWithSign]).

 Outside the trusted base, we mechanize SHA-512 random-oracle reductions from both joint assumptions to standalone X25519 Gap-DH for libsignal’s randomized XEdDSA [95 [#bib-thormarker2021ed25519], 35 [#bib-brendel2021ed25519], 97 [#bib-vcvio]] under an abstract prime-order group-algebra witness (XeddsaCurveLaws [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/Computational/Proofs/SignalPqxdh/XeddsaRom/Statements.lean#L315], §C.10 [#app-xeddsa-rom]). These are a single-run reduction for GapDhWithSignRo [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/Computational/Proofs/SignalPqxdh/XeddsaRom/Statements.lean#L217] with a factor-2 loss from guessing the Edwards sign bit, a two-run DDH-checked variant with factor 1, and a rewinding Forking-Lemma reduction for GapSigEufCmaRo [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/Computational/Proofs/SignalPqxdh/XeddsaRom/Statements.lean#L263] with a 2(qH+1) AdvGapDH(B2)\sqrt{2(q_H+1)\,\mathsf{Adv}^{\mathrm{GapDH}}(B_2)}2(qH​+1)AdvGapDH(B2​)​ bound. Lemma 1 [#lem-pqxdh] and Theorem 2 [#thm-main] retain GapDhWithSign [Tier-1___-statements/Statement-files-_LPAR_T_RPAR_/Computational.Specifications.Assumptions.Assumptions/#SignalPqxdh___GapDhWithSign] and GapSigEufCma [Tier-1___-statements/Statement-files-_LPAR_T_RPAR_/Computational.Specifications.Assumptions.Assumptions/#SignalPqxdh___GapSigEufCma] directly, for three reasons: βKI\beta_{\mathsf{KI}}βKI​ avoids the square-root degradation of the Forking Lemma across NidN_{\mathrm{id}}Nid​ identities, each reduction in the theorem remains a single-run program in the costed language without rewinding, and the theorem avoids assuming a SHA-512 random oracle or concrete Curve25519/Ed25519 field arithmetic.

 Environment. The statement assumes functional properties of the hooked ML-KEM-768, AES-256-CBC and X25519 routines, such as that decryption inverts encryption (§C.7 [#app-wf] and §C.8 [#app-prem]). Everything else about the environment is part of the trusted base (§5.3 [#sec-tcb]):

 the hooked routines of §4 [#sec-interface] (low-level cryptography, with AES-256-CBC including padding hooked as one function; error-message formatting; three libc memory and string routines, memmove, bcmp and strlen; the two functions that write files and standard output; and the remaining non-crypto Rust library routines) behave as their Lean specifications say, write only their declared outputs (no other caller-saved register), and fail only as specified (e.g., no full disk);

 the clock reads the UNIX epoch;

 each call is a fresh, non-setuid process at a fixed load address (no ASLR), with only standard input, output and error open, an empty environment, an 8 MiB stack, 1 GiB of available memory and fixed kernel random bytes (§4.1 [#sec-binary-guarantees]); and

 spqr’s CPU-feature probe reports no AES or PMULL instructions, so the proofs follow the portable code paths.

 C.6 Restrictions in Detail

 Adversary and game. Besides the restrictions in §5.2.5 [#sec-limitations], receive coins are honest, the responder does not send first in the first epoch, and injected ratchet keys must lie in the prime-order group. A few adversarial actions (revealing any signed-prekey secret, or corrupting the initiator’s identity key of any session before its responder accepts) make all later ratchet queries, in every session, return a default answer. Wins recorded before such an action still count, and the handshake test remains available. However, the theorem does not make claims about attacks that combine such an action with queries to an unrelated session. The handshake game is the variant of [52 [#bib-fg24]] with registered secret keys, no randomness reveals and a peer that is uncorrupted at session start. Smaller restrictions are listed in the artifact’s deviation log.

 Setting. Budgets are fixed per instance. There is no self-session, and Bob’s Kyber prekey is always a last-resort key. With 8-byte MACs, the statistical terms are multiples of μ≥2−64\mu\ge 2^{-64}μ≥2−64 that grow polynomially in qkq_kqk​ and qepq_{\mathrm{ep}}qep​ (quadratically in each, per session, and summed over sessions; see §5.2.5 [#sec-limitations] and §C.9 [#app-sess]).

 Binary. Prekey messages are covered only in canonical protobuf encoding. The requirement of the promotion oracle that the bundle match published keys is argued, not mechanized, to lose no generality. The verified executable, on which both the computational and the symbolic theorems are stated, is built with rustc’s release profile with one codegen unit and panic=abort, linked statically with stock LLVM ld.lld (--no-relax) at fixed base address 0x400000 (with external linker resolution checked in CI by alink_check.py); each hooked routine is intercepted at entry by its contract (with libc memmove/bcmp/strlen and the two output routines linked from libc.a and libsignal-cli-io, and the remaining unlinked hooked routines and 18 panic entry points linked as four-byte zero stubs, which trap if executed unhooked). Each run starts with SPQR’s CPU-feature probe already set to report no AES or PMULL support, so SPQR takes its software code paths.

 Cost model. The wrapper that refuses the corruptions the game forbids, and the glue that runs the reductions on the adversary, are plain Lean functions rather than programs in the reduction language; they are part of the trusted base. All other reductions in the statement, including the simulators for Figure 8 of ACD19, are cost-bounded.

 C.7 Well-Formedness of the Handshake Primitives

 Lemma 1 [#lem-pqxdh] quantifies over primitives that satisfy the following conditions. They quantify over all inputs, in some cases restricted to canonical, on-curve or correctly sized ones as stated; in particular the adversary may supply any 32 bytes as an X25519 public key, including small-order and twist points. That the real primitives satisfy them is a trusted premise.

 W1 Lengths and totality. Every primitive returns outputs of the expected byte lengths (hashes, AES blocks, X25519 values, scalars, Edwards points, Kyber1024 keys and ciphertexts), Kyber1024 encapsulation and decapsulation succeed on well-sized inputs, and SPQR initialization always succeeds.

 W2 Agreement with reference definitions. SHA-512, the HKDF that derives the session key, the root and chain key derivations, message-key derivation, AES-256-CBC with HMAC, XEdDSA verification and the canonicity check on public keys agree with reference definitions written in Lean.

 W3 Algebraic laws of X25519. X25519 is invariant under scalar clamping and commutes on base-point products; a product of the base point never yields the all-zero output; whether an output is all-zero does not depend on the scalar; the base point is canonical and canonical inputs give canonical outputs; multiplication by the cofactor 8 commutes with X25519; the signing public key is the X25519 public key; and the Gap-DH oracle agrees with its reference predicate, is symmetric, and is consistent with X25519 in either argument.

 W4 Cofactor projection. The projection onto the prime-order subgroup is idempotent; X25519 outputs depend on a public key only through its projection, and conversely equal outputs under one scalar imply equal projections; scalars with the same public key agree on every on-curve input; the projection is injective on canonical keys; and only 32-byte strings project onto the projection of a public key.

 W5 Correctness. AES-256 block decryption inverts encryption, the AEAD decrypts what it encrypts, XEdDSA signatures verify, and SPQR’s initial send state has a matching receive state with the same salt; every salt SPQR’s receive initialization produces, on any input, has at most 32 bytes.

 W6 Key collisions. A fresh X25519 public key has the same projection as a given one with probability at most 2−2502^{-250}2−250, and a fresh Kyber1024 public key equals a given one with probability at most 2−2552^{-255}2−255. (The bound 2−2502^{-250}2−250 rather than 2−2512^{-251}2−251 is tight for clamped scalars. The bound 2−2552^{-255}2−255 rather than 2−2562^{-256}2−256 is needed because the Kyber1024 public key depends only on the 32-byte seed ddd, through G=SHA3-512G=\mathrm{SHA3\text{-}512}G=SHA3-512. If GGG is a random function, some two of the 22562^{256}2256 seeds collide with probability 1−e−1/2≈0.391-e^{-1/2}\approx 0.391−e−1/2≈0.39, and then the bound 2−2562^{-256}2−256 per target key, a property of the fixed function GGG, fails; three seeds collide only with probability about 2−258.62^{-258.6}2−258.6, so the bound 2−2552^{-255}2−255 holds almost surely.) These give the two collision terms of βKI\beta_{\mathsf{KI}}βKI​.

 Kyber1024’s decapsulation correctness is not among these conditions; it enters Lemma 1 [#lem-pqxdh] separately, as the bound δkem\delta_{\mathrm{kem}}δkem​.

 C.8 Primitive and X25519 Premises

 The Diffie-Hellman group is abstract: a type Pt\mathsf{Pt}Pt with a zero element, a base point base\mathsf{base}base, a function dh:{0,1}256×Pt→Pt\mathsf{dh}:\{0,1\}^{256}\times\mathsf{Pt}\to\mathsf{Pt}dh:{0,1}256×Pt→Pt, an encoding enc:Pt→{0,1}256\mathsf{enc}:\mathsf{Pt}\to\{0,1\}^{256}enc:Pt→{0,1}256 used inside the KDFs, and the encoding encT\mathsf{encT}encT/decoding decT\mathsf{decT}decT of public keys in libsignal’s wire format. Lemma 2 [#lem-session] and Theorem 2 [#thm-main] assume the following (P1–P3 concern ML-KEM-768, AES and the message length; P4–P10 concern X25519).

 P1 ML-KEM-768’s first encapsulation step succeeds on every 64-byte header (Encaps1Total [Tier-2___-definitions/Computational___Specifications___/Computational.Specifications.Session.StatementComposed/#CryptoVerification___E2E___Encaps1Total]; libcrux checks only the length).

 For clamped x-only X25519 on the prime-order subgroup Pt [Tier-2___-definitions/LibsignalBinary___Specifications___/LibsignalBinary.Specifications.LibsignalSpqrV1.Poly/#CryptoVerification___LibsignalSpqrV1___Pt] (0 plus the (ℓ - 1) / 2 pairs {±P} of order ℓ = 2 ^ 252 + 27742317777372353535851937790883648493), every clamped scalar c = 8m (2 ^ 251 ≤ m < 2 ^ 252) satisfies gcd(c, ℓ) = 1, so Q ↦ u(c · Q) is a bijection on Pt [Tier-2___-definitions/LibsignalBinary___Specifications___/LibsignalBinary.Specifications.LibsignalSpqrV1.Poly/#CryptoVerification___LibsignalSpqrV1___Pt] (δ = 2 ^ (-250)). The carrier Pt [Tier-2___-definitions/LibsignalBinary___Specifications___/LibsignalBinary.Specifications.LibsignalSpqrV1.Poly/#CryptoVerification___LibsignalSpqrV1___Pt] must be restricted to this prime-order subgroup under clamped scalars: nonzero E[8] or twist E'[4] torsion points are annihilated by 8m (which violates dh x Q ≠ 0), mixed-order or twist cosets separate uniform Pt [Tier-2___-definitions/LibsignalBinary___Specifications___/LibsignalBinary.Specifications.LibsignalSpqrV1.Poly/#CryptoVerification___LibsignalSpqrV1___Pt] challenges from honest basepoint multiples in CKA.corrGame, and unclamped scalars fail at x ≡ 0 (mod ℓ).

The reductions RA,RPR^{\mathrm{A}},R^{\mathrm{P}}RA,RP (Figure 8 authenticity and privacy), RHR^{\mathrm{H}}RH (hidden salt) and Rhop⋅R^{\mathrm{hop}\cdot}Rhop⋅ are members of DDD; in HS they run behind two further members of DDD, the deliver-forge simulator and a challenge simulator. The statistical terms are suprema over fixed tests, not over adversaries:

 μ\muμ: the probability that a fixed wire verifies under a uniform message key (8-byte HMAC tag), maximized over identities, wires shorter than 2322^{32}232 bytes and tags; μ≥2−64\mu\ge 2^{-64}μ≥2−64.

 μ0\mu_0μ0​: the same for a uniform chain key after ccc steps, maximized over c<232c<2^{32}c<232, salt, identities and the wire; γforge=q μ0\gamma_{\mathrm{forge}}=q\,\mu_0γforge​=qμ0​.

 σ\sigmaσ (σPt\sigma_{\mathrm{Pt}}σPt​): q(qep+1)q(q_{\mathrm{ep}}+1)q(qep​+1) times the supremum, over keys, points and MAC tests, of the PRG advantage of the test against a spurious DH step of libsignal on an evicted epoch’s key (on an arbitrary non-zero point) plus the probability that the test accepts a uniform key.

 γfresh\gamma_{\mathrm{fresh}}γfresh​: qqq times the analogous supremum for HKDF-Expand.

 C.10 SHA-512 Random-Oracle Reductions for XEdDSA

 Outside the trusted base, we mechanize SHA-512 random-oracle reductions from GapDhWithSign [Tier-1___-statements/Statement-files-_LPAR_T_RPAR_/Computational.Specifications.Assumptions.Assumptions/#SignalPqxdh___GapDhWithSign] and GapSigEufCma [Tier-1___-statements/Statement-files-_LPAR_T_RPAR_/Computational.Specifications.Assumptions.Assumptions/#SignalPqxdh___GapSigEufCma] to standalone X25519 Gap-DH modulo the cofactor 8 for libsignal’s randomized XEdDSA signer xeddsaSignRef [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/Computational/Proofs/SignalPqxdh/XeddsaRom/Statements.lean#L60] [79 [#bib-perrin2016xeddsa], 95 [#bib-thormarker2021ed25519], 35 [#bib-brendel2021ed25519]]. Let

 ℓ=2252+27742317777372353535851937790883648493\ell = 2^{252} + 27742317777372353535851937790883648493ℓ=2252+27742317777372353535851937790883648493

 be the Curve25519/Ed25519 subgroup prime. Under §C.7 [#app-wf] and an abstract prime-order group-algebra witness XeddsaCurveLaws [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/Computational/Proofs/SignalPqxdh/XeddsaRom/Statements.lean#L315] pairing the Montgomery and Edwards encodings over Z/ℓZ\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}Z/ℓZ, XeddsaSignRoundtrip [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/Computational/Proofs/SignalPqxdh/XeddsaRom/Statements.lean#L345] proves sign-then-verify correctness both for concrete SHA-512 and in the lazy SHA-512 random-oracle model, and shows that instantiating signing with xeddsaSignRef [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/Computational/Proofs/SignalPqxdh/XeddsaRom/Statements.lean#L60] preserves §C.7 [#app-wf].

 Loss terms and reduction bounds. Let rwide=2512 mod ℓr_{\mathrm{wide}} = 2^{512} \bmod \ellrwide​=2512modℓ and let δwide=rwide(ℓ−rwide)/(ℓ⋅2512)<2−261\delta_{\mathrm{wide}} = r_{\mathrm{wide}}(\ell - r_{\mathrm{wide}}) / (\ell \cdot 2^{512}) < 2^{-261}δwide​=rwide​(ℓ−rwide​)/(ℓ⋅2512)<2−261 be the total-variation distance between a 512-bit SHA-512 digest reduced modulo ℓ\ellℓ and uniform Z/ℓZ\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}Z/ℓZ. For an adversary making qroq_{\mathrm{ro}}qro​ direct SHA-512 queries, qverq_{\mathrm{ver}}qver​ verification queries, qpsigq_{\mathrm{psig}}qpsig​ primitive signing queries, and qSq_SqS​ challenger signing queries, let qH=qro+qver+qpsigq_H = q_{\mathrm{ro}} + q_{\mathrm{ver}} + q_{\mathrm{psig}}qH​=qro​+qver​+qpsig​ be the effective challenge-hash budget, and define the zero-knowledge signing simulation loss and challenge modular-reduction loss:

 Δsim(qS,qH)=qS(2qH+3qS)2512+qS δwide+qS(qS+qH)ℓ,Δmod(qH)=(qH+1) δwide.\begin{aligned}\Delta_{\mathrm{sim}}(q_S, q_H) &= \frac{q_S(2q_H + 3q_S)}{2^{512}} + q_S\,\delta_{\mathrm{wide}} + \frac{q_S(q_S + q_H)}{\ell}, \\ \Delta_{\mathrm{mod}}(q_H) &= (q_H + 1)\,\delta_{\mathrm{wide}}.\end{aligned}Δsim​(qS​,qH​)Δmod​(qH​)​=2512qS​(2qH​+3qS​)​+qS​δwide​+ℓqS​(qS​+qH​)​,=(qH​+1)δwide​.​

 In the SHA-512 random-oracle games GapDhWithSignRo [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/Computational/Proofs/SignalPqxdh/XeddsaRom/Statements.lean#L217] and GapSigEufCmaRo [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/Computational/Proofs/SignalPqxdh/XeddsaRom/Statements.lean#L263], programming the Fiat–Shamir oracle yields two reductions from GapDhWithSignRo [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/Computational/Proofs/SignalPqxdh/XeddsaRom/Statements.lean#L217] to standalone X25519 Gap-DH: a single-run reduction B1B_1B1​ (GapDhWithSignRoBound1A [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/Computational/Proofs/SignalPqxdh/XeddsaRom/Statements.lean#L359], qddh(B1)≤qddh(A)q_{\mathrm{ddh}}(B_1) \le q_{\mathrm{ddh}}(A)qddh​(B1​)≤qddh​(A), qprim(B1)≤qprim(A)q_{\mathrm{prim}}(B_1) \le q_{\mathrm{prim}}(A)qprim​(B1​)≤qprim​(A)) that guesses the challenge key’s Edwards sign bit, and a two-run reduction B1′B_1'B1′​ (GapDhWithSignRoBound1B [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/Computational/Proofs/SignalPqxdh/XeddsaRom/Statements.lean#L372], qddh(B1′)≤2qddh(A)+1q_{\mathrm{ddh}}(B_1') \le 2q_{\mathrm{ddh}}(A) + 1qddh​(B1′​)≤2qddh​(A)+1, qprim(B1′)≤2qprim(A)q_{\mathrm{prim}}(B_1') \le 2q_{\mathrm{prim}}(A)qprim​(B1′​)≤2qprim​(A)) that checks its first candidate via the decisional DH oracle:

 AdvROGapDH+Sign(A)≤2 AdvGapDH(B1)+Δsim(qS,qH),AdvROGapDH+Sign(A)≤AdvGapDH(B1′)+Δsim(qS,qH).\begin{aligned}\mathsf{Adv}^{\mathrm{GapDH+Sign}}_{\mathrm{RO}}(A) &\le 2\,\mathsf{Adv}^{\mathrm{GapDH}}(B_1) + \Delta_{\mathrm{sim}}(q_S, q_H), \\ \mathsf{Adv}^{\mathrm{GapDH+Sign}}_{\mathrm{RO}}(A) &\le \mathsf{Adv}^{\mathrm{GapDH}}(B_1') + \Delta_{\mathrm{sim}}(q_S, q_H).\end{aligned}AdvROGapDH+Sign​(A)AdvROGapDH+Sign​(A)​≤2AdvGapDH(B1​)+Δsim​(qS​,qH​),≤AdvGapDH(B1′​)+Δsim​(qS​,qH​).​

 For GapSigEufCmaRo [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/Computational/Proofs/SignalPqxdh/XeddsaRom/Statements.lean#L263], rewinding AAA at its target challenge query via the VCVio general Forking Lemma [97 [#bib-vcvio]] (qddh(B2)≤2qddh(A)q_{\mathrm{ddh}}(B_2) \le 2q_{\mathrm{ddh}}(A)qddh​(B2​)≤2qddh​(A), qprim(B2)≤2qprim(A)+1q_{\mathrm{prim}}(B_2) \le 2q_{\mathrm{prim}}(A) + 1qprim​(B2​)≤2qprim​(A)+1), extracting the secret scalar modulo ℓ\ellℓ up to sign by 2-special soundness, and lifting it to a clamped 32-byte X25519 scalar establishes both the quadratic bound (GapSigEufCmaRoBoundQuad [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/Computational/Proofs/SignalPqxdh/XeddsaRom/Statements.lean#L384])

 ε′(ε′qH+1−1ℓ)  ≤  2 AdvGapDH(B2),ε′=max⁡(0, AdvROSig+DDH(A)−Δsim(qS,qH)−Δmod(qH)),\varepsilon'\Bigl(\frac{\varepsilon'}{q_H+1} - \frac{1}{\ell}\Bigr) \;\le\; 2\,\mathsf{Adv}^{\mathrm{GapDH}}(B_2), \qquad \varepsilon' = \max\bigl(0,\, \mathsf{Adv}^{\mathrm{Sig+DDH}}_{\mathrm{RO}}(A) - \Delta_{\mathrm{sim}}(q_S, q_H) - \Delta_{\mathrm{mod}}(q_H)\bigr),ε′(qH​+1ε′​−ℓ1​)≤2AdvGapDH(B2​),ε′=max(0,AdvROSig+DDH​(A)−Δsim​(qS​,qH​)−Δmod​(qH​)),

 and the closed-form bound (GapSigEufCmaRoBound [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/Computational/Proofs/SignalPqxdh/XeddsaRom/Statements.lean#L399])

 AdvROSig+DDH(A)  ≤  2(qH+1) AdvGapDH(B2)+qH+1ℓ+Δsim(qS,qH)+Δmod(qH).\mathsf{Adv}^{\mathrm{Sig+DDH}}_{\mathrm{RO}}(A) \;\le\; \sqrt{2(q_H+1)\,\mathsf{Adv}^{\mathrm{GapDH}}(B_2)} + \frac{q_H+1}{\ell} + \Delta_{\mathrm{sim}}(q_S, q_H) + \Delta_{\mathrm{mod}}(q_H).AdvROSig+DDH​(A)≤2(qH​+1)AdvGapDH(B2​)​+ℓqH​+1​+Δsim​(qS​,qH​)+Δmod​(qH​).

 A concrete group model over Z/ℓZ\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}Z/ℓZ witnesses joint satisfiability of §C.7 [#app-wf] and XeddsaCurveLaws [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/Computational/Proofs/SignalPqxdh/XeddsaRom/Statements.lean#L315], and known-answer tests over GF(2255−19)\mathrm{GF}(2^{255}-19)GF(2255−19) with FIPS 180-4 SHA-512 check byte-exact agreement with libsignal v0.105.4 test vectors.

 Model boundaries. Four boundaries separate these standalone reductions from Lemma 1 [#lem-pqxdh] and Theorem 2 [#thm-main]: (a) §C.7 [#app-wf] does not pin the primitive signing function to xeddsaSignRef [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/Computational/Proofs/SignalPqxdh/XeddsaRom/Statements.lean#L60], since libsignal-cli [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/third_party/libsignal/libsignal-session/cli/src/lib.rs#L168-L179] only verifies received bundle signatures; (b) XeddsaCurveLaws [https://github.com/google-deepmind/code-verification/blob/808b4b7ffef198e6a91f71ed776d641a1c236882/Computational/Proofs/SignalPqxdh/XeddsaRom/Statements.lean#L315] is not proved for concrete GF(2255−19)\mathrm{GF}(2^{255}-19)GF(2255−19) Curve25519/Ed25519 arithmetic, as curve25519-dalek lies outside the binary proof scope (§5.2.4 [#sec-assm]); (c) the primitive bridge in the binary theorem instantiates SHA-512 as the concrete Merkle–Damgård hash function rather than a lazy random oracle; and (d) the 2(qH+1) AdvGapDH(B2)\sqrt{2(q_H+1)\,\mathsf{Adv}^{\mathrm{GapDH}}(B_2)}2(qH​+1)AdvGapDH(B2​)​ Forking-Lemma loss makes the signature reduction qualitative rather than concrete-tight at deployment budgets (Nid=220N_{\mathrm{id}}= 2^{20}Nid​=220, qH=264q_H = 2^{64}qH​=264).

 2 For most terms, Sess\mathsf{Sess}Sess charges the advantage of one reduction that samples ooo and runs the per-ooo reduction RoR_oRo​. With δo\delta_oδo​ the signed advantage of RoR_oRo​, this is ∣Eoδo∣|\mathbb{E}_o\delta_o|∣Eo​δo​∣. Per-ooo bounds averaged inside the composition give only Eo∣δo∣≥∣Eoδo∣\mathbb{E}_o|\delta_o|\ge|\mathbb{E}_o\delta_o|Eo​∣δo​∣≥∣Eo​δo​∣, which is not the advantage of any single reduction. The hidden-salt terms of Sess\mathsf{Sess}Sess are of this weaker, averaged form (§C.9 [#app-sess]). ↩ [#fnref-2]
